### 2. Answer and Explanation become independent

Today they are one switch with two reaches (`toggleExplanation` at
`Drill.tsx:3625-3640` hides the Answer when Explanation closes, and opening
Explanation goes through the reveal path; the drawer and Choice Notes also
gate on `currentRevealed`).

- New per-qid state `explanationOpenByQid: Map<string, boolean>`. In the
  `reviewControlsAvailable` modes,
  `explanationOpen = !!explanationOpenByQid.get(qid)` and the explanation
  fetch is enabled by `explanationOpen`; elsewhere both stay
  `notesOpen && drawerTab === 'explanation'` and `currentRevealed`, as now.
  The strip receives `onShowAnswer` / `onExplanation` bound to the new
----
  content renders when `explanationOpen` is true for the current qid;
  otherwise it shows "Press Explanation to open the written explanation."
  and nothing else. Selecting the tab never turns Explanation on.
- **Which picks count as an attempt for the confirm.** `pickModel ===
  'list'`: a pick is already an attempt, so a pill press with a pick on
  screen just flips; no dialog. `pickModel === 'commit'`: a pill press with
  an uncommitted pick first awaits `commitPendingSelection` (same function
  the arrows use) and flips only on `ok`. `adaptive`: unchanged, an unsubmitted pick is not
  an attempt, so the unscored confirm fires and, as today, the commit
  handlers refuse a Submit on an unscored qid. In no mode can a student
  reveal, then submit a scored answer.
- `DrillExplanationTab.revealed` and `OfficialQuestionPane.inlineNotes`
  are driven by `explanationOpen`. The Choice Note dropdowns render when
  `inlineNotes` is non-null, independent of `answerRevealed`. Green/red
  colouring is driven by `answerRevealed` alone. The four combinations
  (off/off, Answer only, Explanation only, both) each have a test.
- **Correctness is a fact, colouring is a state.** `InlineChoiceExplanation`
  receives `isCorrect = correctAnswer === key` (factual). The pane's
  existing `isCorrect` (which folds in `answerRevealed`) is renamed
  `showCorrectColour` and used only for colour and the Check/X icon.

----
  `answerRevealed`; it is set `true` the first time either pill is turned
  on for that qid, so the record says the answer was seen before the change.
  The Run Record snapshot (`:868-887`) gains an optional `answer_revealed`
  per attempt so that fact survives a refresh (optional field, fallback
  `false`, no migration).
- `commit` model: a later pick on an already-persisted question is dropped
  on leave, as today (`commitPendingSelection` returns early on
  `currentAttempted`). Unchanged.
- `adaptive`: after Submit a click changes the highlighted choice only;
  the commit handlers refuse a second Submit on an attempted qid, as today.
  Unchanged.
----
- The drawer's Explanation tab stays, rendered from the same data.

### 8. Choice Notes as dropdowns

- `InlineChoiceExplanation` gains `open: boolean` and `onToggle: () => void`.
  Header row (tag chip, "% of students", "Your answer") always renders when
  any of the three exists. The chevron (`ChevronUp`/`ChevronDown`,
  `aria-expanded`, "Hide explanation for A" / "Show explanation for A") and
  the prose render **only when `note.trim().length > 0`**. A selected
  choice with no published note gets its "Your answer" chip and no
  dropdown; `isInlineChoiceEmpty` keeps its job.
- The choice grid gains a 4th column so the chevron aligns under the
  Check/X icon column (shared with the Restore button from step 3).
- `OfficialQuestionPane` owns `openLetters: Set<string>`, reset to every
  letter with a trimmed note whenever `inlineNotes` becomes non-null or the
  qid changes (pressing Explanation always opens expanded, per the
  recording). Turning Explanation off discards it.
- The "ALWAYS VISIBLE" comment at the top of `InlineChoiceExplanation` is
  rewritten to state the new rule and why.

---
name: watch
description: Watch a video (URL or local path). Downloads with yt-dlp, extracts auto-scaled frames with ffmpeg, pulls the transcript from captions (or Whisper API fallback), and hands the result to Claude so it can answer questions about what's in the video.
argument-hint: "<video-url-or-path> [question]"
allowed-tools: Bash, Read, AskUserQuestion
homepage: https://github.com/bradautomates/claude-video
repository: https://github.com/bradautomates/claude-video
author: bradautomates
license: MIT
user-invocable: true
---

# /watch — Claude watches a video

You don't have a video input; this skill gives you one. A Python script downloads the video, extracts frames as JPEGs, gets a timestamped transcript (native captions first, then Whisper API as fallback), and prints frame paths. You then `Read` each frame path to see the images and combine them with the transcript to answer the user.

## Step 0 — Setup preflight (runs every `/watch` invocation, silent on success)

**Python interpreter:** every `python3 ...` command in this skill is for macOS/Linux. On **Windows**, substitute `python` — the `python3` command on Windows is the Microsoft Store stub and will not run the script.

Before every `/watch` run, verify that dependencies and an API key are in place:

```bash
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/setup.py" --check
```

This is a <100ms lookup. On exit 0, the script emits **nothing** — proceed to Step 1 without comment. **Do NOT announce "setup is complete" to the user** — they don't need a status message on every turn. The only acceptable user-visible output from Step 0 is when remediation is required.

On non-zero exit, follow the table:

| Exit | Meaning | Action |
|------|---------|--------|
| `2` | Missing binaries (`ffmpeg` / `ffprobe` / `yt-dlp`) | Run installer |
| `3` | No Whisper API key | Run installer to scaffold `.env`, then ask user for a key |
| `4` | Both missing | Run installer, then ask for a key |

The installer is idempotent — safe to re-run:

```bash
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/setup.py"
```

On macOS with Homebrew, it auto-installs `ffmpeg` and `yt-dlp`. On Linux/Windows, it prints the exact install commands for the user to run. It scaffolds `~/.config/watch/.env` with commented placeholders at `0600` perms, and writes `SETUP_COMPLETE=true` once deps + a key are in place so the next session knows this user has already been through the wizard.

**If an API key is still missing after install:** use `AskUserQuestion` to ask the user whether they have a Groq API key (preferred — cheaper, faster) or an OpenAI key. Then write it into `~/.config/watch/.env` — set the matching `GROQ_API_KEY=...` or `OPENAI_API_KEY=...` line. If they don't want to set up Whisper, proceed with `--no-whisper` and tell them videos without native captions will come back frames-only.

**Structured mode (optional):** `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/setup.py" --json` emits `{status, first_run, missing_binaries, whisper_backend, has_api_key, config_file, platform}` where `status` is one of `ready | needs_install | needs_key | needs_install_and_key`. Use this when you need to branch on specifics (e.g. "is this the user's very first run?" → `first_run: true`).

Within a single session, you can skip Step 0 on follow-up `/watch` calls — once `--check` returned 0, nothing about the environment changes between turns.

## When to use

- User pastes a video URL (YouTube, Vimeo, X, TikTok, Twitch clip, most yt-dlp-supported sites) and asks about it.
- User points at a local video file (`.mp4`, `.mov`, `.mkv`, `.webm`, etc.) and asks about it.
- User types `/watch <url-or-path> [question]`.

## Recommended limits

- **Best accuracy: videos under 10 minutes.** Frame coverage scales inversely with duration.
- **Hard caps: 100 frames total and 2 fps.** Token cost grows with frame count, so the script targets a frame budget by duration (and never exceeds 2 fps even when the budget would imply more):
  - ≤30s → ~1-2 fps (up to 30 frames)
  - 30s-1min → ~40 frames
  - 1-3min → ~60 frames
  - 3-10min → ~80 frames
  - \>10min → 100 frames, sparsely spaced (warning printed)
- If the user hands you a long video, consider asking whether they want a specific section before burning tokens on a sparse scan.

## How to invoke

**Step 1 — parse the user input.** Separate the video source (URL or path) from any question the user asked. Example: `/watch https://youtu.be/abc what language is this in?` → source = `https://youtu.be/abc`, question = `what language is this in?`.

**Step 2 — run the watch script.** Pass the source verbatim. Do not shell-escape it yourself beyond normal quoting:

```bash
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/watch.py" "<source>"
```

Optional flags:
- `--start T` / `--end T` — focus on a section. Accepts `SS`, `MM:SS`, or `HH:MM:SS`. When either is set, fps auto-scales denser (see "Focusing on a section" below).
- `--max-frames N` — lower the cap for tighter token budget (e.g. `--max-frames 40`)
- `--resolution W` — change frame width in px (default 512; bump to 1024 only if the user needs to read on-screen text)
- `--fps F` — override auto-fps (clamped to 2 fps max)
- `--out-dir DIR` — keep working files somewhere specific (default: an auto-generated tmp dir)
- `--whisper groq|openai` — force a specific Whisper backend (default: prefer Groq if both keys exist)
- `--no-whisper` — disable the Whisper fallback entirely (frames-only if no captions)

### Focusing on a section (higher frame rate)

When the user asks about a specific moment — "what happens at the 2 minute mark?", "zoom into 0:45 to 1:00", "the first 10 seconds" — pass `--start` and/or `--end`. The script switches to focused-mode budgets, which are denser than full-video budgets (still capped at 2 fps):

- ≤5s → 2 fps (up to 10 frames)
- 5-15s → 2 fps (up to 30 frames)
- 15-30s → ~2 fps (up to 60 frames)
- 30-60s → ~1.3 fps (up to 80 frames)
- 60-180s → ~0.6 fps (100 frames, capped)

Focused mode is the right call for:
- Any moment/range the user names explicitly ("around 2:30", "the intro", "the last 30 seconds").
- Any video longer than ~10 minutes where the user's question is about a specific part — running focused on the relevant section is far more useful than a sparse scan of the whole thing.
- Re-runs after a full scan didn't have enough detail in some region.

Transcript is auto-filtered to the same range. Frame timestamps are absolute (real video timeline, not offset-from-start).

Examples:
```bash
# Last 10 seconds of a 1 minute video
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/watch.py" video.mp4 --start 50 --end 60

# Zoom into 2:15 → 2:45 at 3 fps (90 frames)
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/watch.py" "$URL" --start 2:15 --end 2:45 --fps 3

# From 1h12m to the end of the video
python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/watch.py" "$URL" --start 1:12:00
```

**Step 3 — Read every frame path the script lists.** The Read tool renders JPEGs directly as images for you. Read all frames in a single message (parallel tool calls) so you see them together. The frames are in chronological order with a `t=MM:SS` timestamp so you can align them to the transcript.

**Step 4 — answer the user.** You now have two streams of evidence:
- **Frames** — what's on screen at each timestamp
- **Transcript** — what's said at each timestamp. The report's header shows the source (`captions` = yt-dlp pulled native subs; `whisper (groq)` or `whisper (openai)` = transcribed by API).

If the user asked a specific question, answer it directly citing timestamps. If they didn't ask anything, summarize what happens in the video — structure, key moments, notable visuals, spoken content.

**Step 5 — clean up.** The script prints a working directory at the end. If the user isn't going to ask follow-ups about this video, delete it with `rm -rf <dir>`. If they might, leave it in place.

## Transcription

The script gets a timestamped transcript in one of two ways:

1. **Native captions (free, preferred).** yt-dlp pulls manual or auto-generated subtitles from the source platform if available.
2. **Whisper API fallback.** If no captions came back (or the source is a local file), the script extracts audio (`ffmpeg -vn -ac 1 -ar 16000 -b:a 64k`, ~0.5 MB/min) and uploads it to whichever Whisper API has a key configured:
   - **Groq** — `whisper-large-v3`. Preferred default: cheaper, faster. Get a key at console.groq.com/keys.
   - **OpenAI** — `whisper-1`. Fallback. Get a key at platform.openai.com/api-keys.

Both keys live in `~/.config/watch/.env`. The script prefers Groq when both are set; override with `--whisper openai` to force OpenAI. Use `--no-whisper` to skip the fallback entirely.

## Failure modes and handling

- **Setup preflight failed** → run `python3 "${CLAUDE_SKILL_DIR}/scripts/setup.py"` (auto-installs ffmpeg/yt-dlp via brew on macOS, scaffolds the `.env`). For API key, ask the user via `AskUserQuestion` and write it to `~/.config/watch/.env`.
- **No transcript available** → captions missing AND (no Whisper key OR Whisper API failed). Script prints a hint pointing to setup. Proceed frames-only and tell the user.
- **Long video warning printed** → acknowledge it in your answer. Offer to re-run focused on a specific section via `--start`/`--end` rather than a sparse full-video scan.
- **Download fails** → yt-dlp's error goes to stderr. If it's a login-required or region-locked video, tell the user plainly; do not keep retrying.
- **Whisper request fails** → the error is printed to stderr (likely: invalid key, rate limit, or 25 MB upload limit on a very long video). The report will say "none available" for transcript. You can retry with `--whisper openai` if Groq failed (or vice versa).

## Token efficiency

This skill burns tokens primarily on frames. Order of magnitude:
- 80 frames at 512px wide is roughly 50-80k image tokens depending on aspect ratio.
- The transcript is cheap (a few thousand tokens at most for a 10-minute video).
- Bumping `--resolution` to 1024 roughly quadruples the image tokens per frame. Only do it when necessary.

If you already watched a video this session and the user asks a follow-up, do **not** re-run the script — you already have the frames and transcript in context. Just answer from what you have.

## Security & Permissions

**What this skill does:**
- Runs `yt-dlp` locally to download the video and pull native captions when the source supports them (public data; the request goes directly to whatever host the URL points at)
- Runs `ffmpeg` / `ffprobe` locally to extract frames as JPEGs and, when Whisper is needed, a mono 16 kHz audio clip
- Sends the extracted audio clip to Groq's Whisper API (`api.groq.com/openai/v1/audio/transcriptions`) when `GROQ_API_KEY` is set (preferred — cheaper, faster)
- Sends the extracted audio clip to OpenAI's audio transcription API (`api.openai.com/v1/audio/transcriptions`) when `OPENAI_API_KEY` is set and Groq is not, or when `--whisper openai` is forced
- Writes the downloaded video, frames, audio, and an intermediate transcript to a working directory under the system temp dir (or `--out-dir` if specified) so Claude can `Read` them
- Reads / creates `~/.config/watch/.env` (mode `0600`) to store the Whisper API key(s) and a `SETUP_COMPLETE` marker. As a fallback, also reads `.env` in the current working directory

**What this skill does NOT do:**
- Does not upload the video itself to any API — only the extracted audio goes out, and only when native captions are missing AND Whisper is not disabled with `--no-whisper`
- Does not access any platform account (no login, no session cookies, no posting)
- Does not share API keys between providers (Groq key only goes to `api.groq.com`, OpenAI key only goes to `api.openai.com`)
- Does not log, cache, or write API keys to stdout, stderr, or output files
- Does not persist anything outside the working directory and `~/.config/watch/.env` — clean up the working directory when you're done (Step 5)

**Bundled scripts:** `scripts/watch.py` (entry point), `scripts/download.py` (yt-dlp wrapper), `scripts/frames.py` (ffmpeg frame extraction), `scripts/transcribe.py` (caption selection + Whisper orchestration), `scripts/whisper.py` (Groq / OpenAI clients), `scripts/setup.py` (preflight + installer)

Review scripts before first use to verify behavior.

# Codex-Build — Codex Types, Claude Verifies

The role-flip of `/codex-review`: there, Claude builds the plan and Codex critiques read-only. Here, **Codex is the builder with write access; Claude is the spec-writer and reviewer.** Codex implements a frozen spec end-to-end; Claude judges the diff like a contributor PR, demands proof, and iterates fixes in the same Codex session. The human enters at exactly two points: kickoff and diff sign-off.

Adapted from Peter Steinberger's `codex-first` pattern (agent-scripts), rebuilt on this house's verified Codex mechanics.

**Spec quality decides success.** Codex starts with zero session context — everything it needs must be in the prompt. A plan that survived `/grill-me-codex` or `/codex-review` already is a frozen spec; that's the ideal input.

## Prerequisites (verify once, fast)

- `codex --version` ≥ 0.130 (older CLIs error on the default `gpt-5.5` model).
- Codex authenticated (prior `codex login`; ChatGPT account is fine). On auth/model error, surface it — don't silently retry.
- Do NOT pin `-m` or model config (e.g. `model_reasoning_effort`) unless the user asks. Pinning `gpt-5.x-codex` variants 400s on ChatGPT-account auth; config defaults come from `~/.codex/config.toml`.
- **Echo the active model at kickoff** so the user can confirm: read the `model` line from `~/.codex/config.toml` (absent = "CLI default"); state it with the resolved tunables. If the user objects, stop before launching the build.
- **Codex has a native image-generation tool** in `codex exec` sessions (ChatGPT-account backed, no API key; verified 2026-07-08 — it saved a generated PNG to disk headless). Specs may therefore include "generate these image assets yourself" steps: name exact file paths, dimensions, and style in the prompt contract.
- Run from the target repo's root (both `exec` and `resume` then need no `-C`; `resume` doesn't support `-C` anyway).

## Tunables (read from args, else default)

| Var | Default | Meaning |
|-----|---------|---------|
| `SPEC_FILE` | `PLAN.md` | The frozen spec Codex implements. |
| `MAX_FIX_ROUNDS` | `2` | Fix iterations via resume before Claude takes over and finishes directly. |
| `LOG_FILE` | `PLAN-REVIEW-LOG.md` | Append-only build transcript. If it exists (Act 1/2 ran), append `## Act 3 — Build`; else create it. |
| `PROOF_CMD` | from spec | Exact test/verify command Codex must run as proof. If the spec lacks one, ask the user ONE question to get it before launching. |

Echo resolved values before starting.

## Step 0 — Gates (before any Codex launch)

1. **Spec gate.** `SPEC_FILE` must exist and read as a work order (goal, concrete steps, bounds). No spec → offer `/grill-me-codex` (interview first) or `/codex-review` (have a plan, want it stress-tested) instead. If the user insists on building from a rough idea, write the spec WITH them first — that's design, and design stays with Claude.
2. **Clean-tree gate.** `git status -sb`. Dirty working tree → STOP and ask the user to commit or stash first. Non-negotiable: Codex writes with full access, and a dirty tree means its diff can't be isolated or cleanly reverted.
3. Confirm scope in one line, then go. No round-by-round approvals; the human gate is at the end.

## Step 1 — The build prompt (contract, via temp file)

Never inline-quote the prompt — write it to a temp file. Fill this contract completely; when chained from a grill/review skill, derive it from the plan's sections:

```bash
P=$(mktemp)
cat >"$P" <<'EOF'
GOAL: <one paragraph — what done looks like>
SPEC: Read <SPEC_FILE> at the repo root. It is a frozen, already-reviewed spec.
  Implement it exactly. If a step is impossible as written, implement the
  closest faithful version and report the deviation — do not redesign.
KEY PATHS: <files/dirs Codex will touch or must read first>
CONSTRAINTS: <"don't touch X", style rules, deps that must not change>
NON-GOALS: <explicitly out of scope — from the plan's Out of scope section>
PROOF: Run `<PROOF_CMD>` and include its full output in your report.
OUTPUT: End with a report — files changed (one line each: path + what/why),
  proof output, and any deviations from the spec with reasons.
EOF
```

## Step 2 — Launch Codex (fresh session, capture `thread_id`)

```bash
codex exec --yolo --json -o /tmp/codex-build.txt - <"$P" 2>/dev/null | grep '"type":"thread.started"'
```

- Prompt goes via stdin (`- <"$P"`) — this both avoids quoting bugs AND sidesteps the non-TTY stdin hang (`codex exec` blocks forever waiting on stdin EOF under Claude Code's Bash tool; feeding the file gives immediate EOF).
- Parse `thread_id` from the `{"type":"thread.started","thread_id":"..."}` line → `THREAD_ID`. Codex's final report lands in `/tmp/codex-build.txt` — read that file; don't parse the JSONL stream for content.
- `2>/dev/null` suppresses cosmetic MCP/auth stderr noise. Confirm success by the report file + a `thread.started` line; neither → failed run (auth/model) — stop and tell the user.
- **Timing:** foreground with `timeout: 600000` on the Bash tool call (default 2-min tool timeout kills real builds). If the spec is clearly >10 min of work (multi-file feature, migration, anything with image generation), launch with `run_in_background: true` instead and read the `-o` file when it exits. Don't kill a quiet background run early — Codex builds are legitimately slow.
- **Heads-up on completion (required):** when a background Codex run finishes, the FIRST line of your next message to the user must be a loud standalone banner — `🔔 CODEX FINISHED — <what> (exit ok/fail) — verifying now` — BEFORE any verification output. The user is not watching tool calls; never let a completed build slide silently into the verify phase.

## Step 3 — Verify (Claude, always, never delegated)

Codex's report is advisory. Verify yourself:

1. `git status -sb` + read the FULL diff (`git diff`). Judge it like a contributor PR: correctness, spec fidelity, style match with surrounding code, nothing touched outside scope.
2. Run `PROOF_CMD` yourself (or the focused tests for the changed area). Codex's pasted output doesn't count as proof.
3. Append to `LOG_FILE` under `## Act 3 — Build`: `### Round <n> — Codex build` + its report summary + `### Claude's verdict` + what passed/failed review.

## Step 4 — Fix loop (same session, bounded)

Problems found → resume the SAME session (Codex keeps its context; cheaper and better than a fresh run). Write the fix list to a temp file (`$P2`), same contract discipline: exact problem, exact file, proof expected.

```bash
# resume has no --yolo and no -C: run from the repo dir and spell the long flag,
# or Codex inherits config.toml's sandbox (possibly read-only) and can't write.
codex exec resume "$THREAD_ID" --dangerously-bypass-approvals-and-sandbox --json \
  -o /tmp/codex-build.txt - <"$P2" 2>/dev/null >/dev/null
```

Re-verify (Step 3) after each round. After `MAX_FIX_ROUNDS` failed rounds: STOP delegating — Claude takes over and finishes the remaining fixes directly. Log the takeover. Ping-ponging trivia through delegation burns more than it saves.

## Step 5 — Human gate (diff sign-off)

Present: 3-bullet summary of what was built, files-changed list, proof-test output (pass/fail, verbatim tail), rounds used, any spec deviations. Ask: *"Codex built it, proof passes, diff reviewed. Commit?"*

- Commit ONLY on yes — and Claude writes the commit, never Codex.
- Rejected → ask what's wrong, route back to Step 4 (or take over directly if fix rounds are spent).

## Hard rules

- Clean tree before launch. Always. No exceptions.
- Claude never skips the diff read. Codex claims are advisory until Claude has read the diff and run the proof.
- Fix loop terminates at `MAX_FIX_ROUNDS` — then Claude takes over. No unbounded delegation ping-pong.
- Commits, pushes, releases, GitHub mutations: Claude-side only, after the human gate. Codex never commits.
- `LOG_FILE` is the deliverable — with Acts 1/2 it tells the whole story: grilled → reviewed → built → verified.

## What NOT to do

- Don't build without a spec — that's designing by delegation, and it fails. Route to `/grill-me-codex` or `/codex-review` first.
- Don't use for ~<20-line single-obvious-change edits — just make the edit.
- Don't pin `-codex` model variants on ChatGPT-account auth — 400s.
- Don't resume with `--last` — capture and use the explicit `THREAD_ID` (parallel sessions make `--last` grab the wrong thread). And ECHO the id into the command visibly before running: `resume` with a missing/garbage id can silently fall back to the most recent session instead of erroring (observed 2026-07-08) — a wrong-target resume looks exactly like a successful one.
- Don't parse the JSONL stream for the report — read the `-o` file.
- Don't let Codex commit, and don't auto-commit yourself — human gate first.

### 12. Verify in the browser

Preview server, at 1280, 1024 and 390 widths and with the drawer docked:
- A guided drill: bottom bar is arrows + ovals, no Submit; pick B, press
  Answer → B red, correct green, nothing else; Answer again → colours gone;
  Explanation → breakdown under stimulus, dropdowns open; fold one;
  Explanation off → dropdowns and breakdown gone; right arrow → oval marks
  answered; back arrow → pick and states as left; Reset Response → pick
  cleared, timer running; pick again → the session map shows the new pick
  with a two-entry trail and `answerRevealed: true`; the drawer never
  opened on its own during any of this; End Drill closes out and the
  `attempts` row count for that user + qid grew by exactly one relative to
  a count taken before the drill.
- A filter drill: same, plus arrow persists per question.
- Smart Drill: its own bar unchanged (Submit, Skip, auto-reveal switch);
  pills, breakdown, dropdowns and Reset work; no tutor after a wrong
  Submit.
- Cold cache first open: with an empty React Query cache, press Explanation
  on a fresh question; the slot shows "Loading explanation…" then the
  content, with no layout jump of the stimulus block.
- A sealed run and a full section: selection and navigation unchanged.
- Computed style of the stimulus `div` (font-family, font-size, padding,
  max-width) identical before and after, read with `getComputedStyle`.
- Screenshots for Joshua.

## Out of scope

- Smart Drill's question selection bug and its bottom bar.
- A server-side kill switch on the `tutor-chat` edge function. Edge
  functions do not deploy from a push on this project (memory: prove a
  function deploy with the build marker), so it is its own change; the
  client flag removes every launch point in the product and that is what
  Joshua asked for.
- `TutorChatModal`, token budgeting.
- Sealed runs, practice-test sittings, full-section mode, the classic
  `DrillTopBar` layout: behaviour preserved.
- Reading Comprehension (`RcChoiceDisclosure`) and the Classroom
  `LRQuestionModal`.
- Video explanations: no toggle, no hosting, no claim.
- Authoring the missing explanations.

Tool result:
### 10. Census of questions with no authored explanation

- `scripts/missing-explanations-census.mjs`. Loads the service key the way
  `scripts/db-pull.mjs` does, and **pages every table at 1000 rows with
  `Range` headers until a short page**, the same loop `db-pull.mjs` uses.
  Aborts (non-zero exit, nothing written) on any query error or if a table's
  row count disagrees with a `count=exact` head request.
- Reads the bank from `public/data/PT*-LR.json` (156 files).
- **One predicate, two runtimes, one fixture set.** `hasAuthoredProse` (a
  step or choice-note `body` non-empty after `trim()`) is implemented in
  `useLrExplanation.ts` (replacing the row-count `hasAuthored`) and in the
  script. A vitest spec `explanationProse.fixtures.test.ts` runs **both**
  against the same fixtures (`src/lib/__fixtures__/explanationProse.json`:
  empty, whitespace-only, null, mixed-style, note-only, step-only) by
  importing the `.mjs` predicate directly, so they cannot drift.
- Reasons are exactly what the data proves: `not ingested` (no
  `lr_questions` row for the address) or `no authored prose` (row exists,
  nothing passes the predicate). No "video only" label: nothing in the
  schema records a video; the 14 known video-only questions (PT143, PT146
  per the 2026-08-03 migration) are a subset of "no authored prose", noted
  in the report header.
- Output `ai-os/wiki/explanations/MISSING-EXPLANATIONS.md`: summary counts
  (bank total, ingested, with prose, missing by reason), then one table per
  PrepTest, rows `PT · S · Q · question type · reason`, sorted PT, section,
  question. Run once, committed to the vault, not the app repo.

Tool result:
# Plan: post-question Explanation, LSAT Lab shape, AI Tutor off
_Locked via grill-with-docs on 2026-09-12 by Claude + Joshua. Revised after Codex rounds 1 to 5, re-cut after Joshua's clarification (fixed drills: arrows + bubbles, no Submit), revised again after Codex re-cut rounds 1 and 2 (cap), then Joshua's final rulings: no freeze, Explanation never opens the drawer. Terms per `src/components/drill/CONTEXT.md` (Answer, Explanation, Argument Breakdown, Choice Note, Reset Response, AI Tutor, Answer Navigator, Oval State, Drill Launch, Drill Run Spec)._

## Goal

On the official drill surface (`/drill`), the student reviews a question the
way LSAT Lab shows it. The **Answer** pill and the **Explanation** pill in
the session strip are independent on/off toggles, exactly as in Joshua's
LSAT Lab recording: press to show, press again to hide, and nothing ever
locks the choices. Pressing Explanation shows expanders, not prose: a
chevron on a rule under the stimulus that opens the **Argument Breakdown**,
and a chevron at the right edge of every choice card that opens that
choice's **Choice Note**. Everything starts closed and the student opens
what she wants (Joshua's 2026-09-12 LSAT Lab recording, 13:11). The
drawer's Explanation tab shows the same content but only when the student
opens the drawer herself. A **Reset Response**
control clears the pick. A fixed drill (guided drill, small drill, custom
queue) has no Submit pill: its bottom bar is arrows and ovals, as today,
and nothing shows colour until the student presses Answer. The **AI
Tutor** is off for every student behind one switch, because the Question
Explanation is authored LSAT Lab content already in Postgres
(`lr_explanations`, `lr_explanation_steps`, `lr_explanation_choice_notes`)
and the generated chat was failing on screen. Alongside the code change, a
census of every bank question with no authored prose, keyed PrepTest /
Section / Question.

## How picks work today (the model this plan builds on)

Two pick models exist on the official surface and both stay:

- **List drills** (`isPracticeSetMode`, set at `Drill.tsx:556` for every
  `type-drill` with `selectedQids`: guided drills, assignments, saved sets,
  Intro to LR practice sets, Bootcamp practice). A pick is written straight
  into `session.attempts` with an `answerTrail` and `switchCount`
  (`:1345-1375`); changing the pick is a recorded change of mind; the set
  is persisted at the end (`handleFinishSection` / `handleFinishPracticeSet`).
  The bar's Submit pill is already hidden once a pick exists
  (`primaryVisible={!currentAttempted && !currentUnscored}`, `:4308`).
- **Commit drills** (`type-drill` drawn by `filter`, no `selectedQids`): a
  pick sits in `selectedAnswer` and is persisted per question by
  `commitPendingSelection` when the student presses Next, Previous, an oval
  (`:4297-4326`) or Submit.
- **Smart Drill** (`adaptive`): Submit persists per question; Skip; the
  auto-reveal switch. Joshua reports its question selection is broken; a
  separate job, untouched here.

Both fixed-drill kinds already render arrows + `AnswerOvals` in the bar's
`pager` slot (`:4313`). The ovals mean "which question", never "right or
wrong" (Oval State), and that does not change.

## Scope, classified by Drill Launch kind

`DrillRunSpec` carries no `kind` (ADR 0016: `mode` is not the run kind; six
runs share `type-drill`). Add `launchKind: DrillLaunchBody['kind']` to
`DrillRunSpec`, set in `drillRunSpecFromLaunch`, and classify by it with an
exhaustive switch in a pure helper `src/lib/drillReviewPolicy.ts`:

| launch kind | review policy |
|---|---|
| `guided-drill`, `assignment`, `saved-set`, `questions`, `practice-set`, `bootcamp-day`, `filter` | `fixed` |
| `smart-drill` | `adaptive` |
| `section` | `none` (full section keeps today's opt-in reveal) |
| any kind with `sealed` or a practice-test leg (`ptRun`) | `none` |

`reviewControlsAvailable = policy !== 'none' && isOfficialLayout`.

The **pick model** is a second, independent classification, because a
`questions` launch can arrive as `natural-drill` (`Drill.tsx:548`), which
never sets `isPracticeSetMode`:
`pickModel = hasSelectedQidList(spec) ? 'list' : 'commit'`, where
`hasSelectedQidList` is the exact condition at `:556`
(`mode === 'type-drill' && config.selectedQids?.length > 0`), lifted into
the same pure helper. `'list'` is the in-memory-attempt model,
`'commit'` the per-question-persist model. Both are pure functions of the
resolved spec, so an early render can never misclassify a run (the
`isPracticeSetMode` state stays for the existing paths that read it;
classification no longer depends on it).
Declared **after** `session` (`:307`) and `isPracticeSetMode` (`:471`) and
before `handleAnswerSelect` (`:1329`); the `isOfficialLayout` at `:3751` is
hoisted to the same spot and deleted from the render branch.

**Every parent-level change below is branched on
`reviewControlsAvailable`, with today's code as the else branch**; full
section and a sealed assignment get regression tests on both pills.

## Approach

Work on a fresh branch off `main` (`feat/post-question-explanation`); the
current worktree branch `feat/calendar-strip-deeplink` carries 87 modified
files from another session and none of them are touched here.

### 1. One switch for the AI Tutor

- `src/components/drill/aiTutorFlag.ts` exports `AI_TUTOR_ENABLED = false`
  with a comment naming the surfaces it gates and the date.
- The constant gates **mounting and launching**, not just initial state:
  - `renderTutorOverlay` returns `null` when the flag is off, regardless of
    `tutorChatOpen`.
  - `openTutorForCurrent`, the three wrong-answer launch sites
    (`Drill.tsx:1849`, `:1957`, `:2105`) and the fixed-queue submit wrapper
    (`:4261-4283`) all read `AI_TUTOR_ENABLED && tutorMode`.
    `resolveTutorLaunch` gains a required `flagEnabled` argument and
    returns `shouldOpen: false` when it is off.
  - The legacy layout's tutor controls (`:4416-4431`) render only when the
    flag is on.
  - `tutorMode` initial state stays `true`; the flag is the master.
    `tutorToggleVisible` on the frame stays `false` as today.
- `DrillExplanationTab` receives `onAskTutor` only when the flag is on. Its
  no-prose copy becomes: "No written explanation was published for this
  question."
- `renderVoiceCoachChip` returns `null` when the flag is off.
- `TutorChatModal` and the `tutor-chat` edge function are not edited. The
  flag controls the product surface, not the service; a server-side switch
  is a follow-up (see Out of scope).

### 2. Answer and Explanation become independent

Today they are one switch with two reaches (`toggleExplanation` at
`Drill.tsx:3625-3640` hides the Answer when Explanation closes, and opening
Explanation goes through the reveal path; the drawer and Choice Notes also

Tool result:
Explanation goes through the reveal path; the drawer and Choice Notes also
gate on `currentRevealed`).

- New per-qid state `explanationOpenByQid: Map<string, boolean>`. In the
  `reviewControlsAvailable` modes,
  `explanationOpen = !!explanationOpenByQid.get(qid)` and the explanation
  fetch is enabled by `explanationOpen`; elsewhere both stay
  `notesOpen && drawerTab === 'explanation'` and `currentRevealed`, as now.
  The strip receives `onShowAnswer` / `onExplanation` bound to the new
  handlers only when `reviewControlsAvailable`, else the existing ones.
- `toggleAnswerReveal(qid)`: flips `showAnswerRevealed[qid]` only. Never
  touches the drawer or `explanationOpenByQid`.
- **The pill never touches the drawer, and never asks.** `toggleExplanation(qid)`
  flips the map entry, whatever the pick state: no pick, an uncommitted
  pick, or an attempt. There is **no confirm dialog** on either pill in
  the `reviewControlsAvailable` modes (Joshua, 2026-09-12: "if there's no
  answer committed and you press Answer, it should just show the right
  answer; if you press Explanation, it should show the explanation"). The
  existing `confirmUnscoredReveal` dialog stays only in the else branch
  (full section / sealed / classic). A reveal with no attempt sets
  `answerRevealed` for the qid so the later pick is recorded as made after
  seeing the answer (step 4). It never opens, closes or retabs the drawer
  (Joshua: "they have to click the drawer"; the recording shows the
  sidebar unchanged when Explanation toggles). Same rule for
  `toggleAnswerReveal`.
- **The drawer's Explanation tab is read-only on that state.** Selecting
  it (`onNotesPanelTabChange` at `:4088`) just sets the tab, as today. Its
  content renders when `explanationOpen` is true for the current qid;
  otherwise it shows "Press Explanation to open the written explanation."
  and nothing else. Selecting the tab never turns Explanation on.
- **A pill press never commits a pick.** `pickModel === 'list'`: a pick is
  already an attempt, the press just flips. `pickModel === 'commit'`: the
  press flips and leaves the pick uncommitted; the arrow commits it later
  as today, and because `answerRevealed` is already `true` for the qid the
  persisted attempt carries `answer_revealed: true`. `adaptive`: the press
  flips and silently marks the qid unscored (no dialog); the commit
  handlers refuse a Submit on an unscored qid, as today. In no mode can a
  student reveal, then submit a scored answer.
- `DrillExplanationTab.revealed` and `OfficialQuestionPane.inlineNotes`
  are driven by `explanationOpen`. The Choice Note dropdowns render when
  `inlineNotes` is non-null, independent of `answerRevealed`. Green/red
  colouring is driven by `answerRevealed` alone. The four combinations
  (off/off, Answer only, Explanation only, both) each have a test.
- **Correctness is a fact, colouring is a state.** `InlineChoiceExplanation`
  receives `isCorrect = correctAnswer === key` (factual). The pane's
  existing `isCorrect` (which folds in `answerRevealed`) is renamed
  `showCorrectColour` and used only for colour and the Check/X icon.

### 3. Fixed drills: no Submit pill, no auto-reveal switch, nothing locks

- The fixed-queue `OfficialSmartDrillBar` (`:4255-4330`) stays, with its
  Prev/Next arrows, `AnswerOvals` pager and End. Two props change when the
  policy is `fixed`: `primaryVisible={false}`
  (the Submit pill never shows; arrows and ovals already commit on the way
  out) and `onAutoRevealChange` omitted (the switch is not rendered; the
  `autoReveal` preference is left alone for Smart Drill).
- **One wrapper for leaving a question.** `commitThenNavigate(destination)`
  in `Drill.tsx` replaces the three inline `commitPendingSelection` +
  move blocks (`:4297-4326`) and the End path. The lock is **synchronous**:
  `actionLockRef = useRef(false)` is tested and set before the first
  `await`, and a mirrored `actionInFlight` state exists only to rerender
  the disabled controls. A second arrow, oval or End press while the ref
  is held returns immediately. `commitPendingSelection` returns a
  discriminated result on every path:
  `{ ok: true, attempts }` (persisted, or nothing to persist: `list`
  model, already attempted, unscored, no pick) or `{ ok: false }` (save
  failed). The wrapper moves only on `ok`. `Drill.tsx:2545`
  `handleFinishSection` does not read `selectedAnswer`, so End goes through
  the same wrapper with `destination: 'end'`, which passes
  `result.attempts` to `handleFinishSection`.
  While the lock is held the arrows, ovals, End, both pills, Reset and the
  choices are disabled (the pane gets `interactionLocked`), so the
  committed pick can never differ from the one on screen.
- `answerLocked` and `isRetryAfterWrong` are no longer set by any reveal
  path in the `reviewControlsAvailable` modes (`:1851`, `:1958`, `:1989`,
  `:2113`). They stay legacy-layout concerns.
- `handleAnswerSelect` guard: the `if (!tutorMode && showSolution) return;`
  line becomes `if (!reviewControlsAvailable && !tutorMode && showSolution) return;`.
- `OfficialQuestionPane` gets `choicesStayLive?: boolean` (default `false`).
  When true, choice buttons are disabled only when `isEliminated` or
  `interactionLocked`. `Drill.tsx` passes `choicesStayLive={reviewControlsAvailable}`.
- Cross-out: **Restore is always a real button.** With Answer on, the
  correct choice and the wrong pick show the Check/X verdict icon in the
  3rd grid column (as today); the other three keep their cross-out/Restore
  control there, live. When a verdict choice is *also* eliminated, the pane
  renders a separate labelled `<button aria-label="Restore choice A">`
  (`EyeOff` icon) in the new 4th column, keyboard-focusable; pressing it
  calls `onEliminate(key)`. Test: eliminate the correct choice, turn Answer
  on, Restore is present and works.

### 4. Picks stay live, and the record says the answer was seen

Joshua's ruling: no freeze. After Answer or Explanation has been on, the
choices behave exactly as they did before.

- `list` model: a later pick is a recorded change of mind, as today
  (`answerTrail`, `switchCount`). The attempt already carries
  `answerRevealed`; it is set `true` the first time either pill is turned
  on for that qid, so the record says the answer was seen before the change.
  The Run Record snapshot (`:868-887`) gains an optional `answer_revealed`
  per attempt so that fact survives a refresh (optional field, fallback
  `false`, no migration).
- `commit` model: a later pick on an already-persisted question is dropped
  on leave, as today (`commitPendingSelection` returns early on
  `currentAttempted`). Unchanged.
- `adaptive`: after Submit a click changes the highlighted choice only;
  the commit handlers refuse a second Submit on an attempted qid, as today.
  Unchanged.
- With Answer on, colour follows the live pick: the correct choice is
  green, the highlighted choice is red if it is not the correct one.
- No `practiceByQid`, no `revealedEverByQid`, no `effectiveAnswer`; the
  earlier drafts' practice-state machinery is deleted from this plan.
  `selectedAnswer` and `session.attempts` are the only pick state, as now.

### 5. Reset Response

- `OfficialQuestionPane` gets `onResetResponse?: () => void` and renders a
  right-aligned ghost button "Reset Response" under the `<ul>`, `disabled`
  when `selectedAnswer` is empty or `interactionLocked`. Rendered only when
  the prop is present (i.e. `reviewControlsAvailable`).
- Handler: the existing un-pick path (`handleAnswerSelect` with the
  current answer, `:1333-1343`): `selectedAnswer` cleared; in the `list`
  model the attempt entry is removed and the timer records `''`, which is
  today's recorded change of mind to "no answer". Touches nothing else: not
  the timer's run, `showAnswerRevealed`, `explanationOpenByQid`, drawer,
  journal, or resume. Matches the second recording: pick cleared, Confirm
  greyed, timer still running.

### 6. Persistence is honest and single-flight

- `persistAttempt` (`:1512-1524`) returns a typed result instead of a
  boolean: `{ ok: true } | { ok: false; errorKind: 'auth' | 'network' | 'db' | 'unknown' }`,
  classified inside its `catch` where the error still exists; the
  structured failure event is emitted there. Existing `if (!saved)`
  callers become `if (!saved.ok)`.
- `handleSubmitNonAdaptive` (`:1744-1785`) currently toasts on a failed
  save and still writes `session.attempts` and adaptive stats. Change: on
  `!saved.ok`, toast and `return null` **before** any state write.
- **Every caller honours that null.** `commitPendingSelection` maps it to
  `{ ok: false }`; `commitThenNavigate` (arrows, ovals, End) and the
  `commit`-model pill pre-commit stop there: no move, no completion, the
  question stays with its pick.
- Single-flight lives **inside** `handleSubmitNonAdaptive` (and stays
  inside `commitCurrentAdaptiveAnswer`): `pendingCommitsRef: Map<qid,
  Promise<Map<string, Attempt> | null>>`; a second caller for the same qid
  receives the existing promise; cleared in `finally`. The `actionInFlight`
  lock from step 3 is the single reactive pending state; `pendingCommitQid`
  from the earlier draft is dropped.
- Failure event: `reportCommitFailure({ qid, launchKind, transition, errorKind, deduped })`
  via `console.error` with a structured object (no PII; no user id, no
  answer text). If the repo has an error-reporting sink by build time it is
  used; otherwise `console.error` is the sink and that is stated in the
  code comment.
- Tests: double right-arrow, and right-arrow then End, with a delayed
  `persistAttempt` mock: exactly one `attempts` insert **and exactly one
  navigation/completion** each.

### 7. Argument Breakdown under the stimulus

- New `src/components/drill/official/ArgumentBreakdown.tsx`, props
  `{ data, isLoading, isError, activeStyle, onStyleChange, fontScale,
  open, onToggle }`. **Closed by default.** It renders a centred rule
  (`border-t`) with a small bordered chevron button sitting on the rule
  (`ChevronDown`, `ChevronUp` when open, `aria-expanded`, accessible name
  "Show argument breakdown" / "Hide argument breakdown"), exactly LSAT
  Lab's expander under the stimulus. When open, below the rule: the style
  pills only when `styles.length > 1`, then labelled steps (label on its
  own line, slate-700 600-weight ~0.85 × base; body sans at base size, 1.6
  line-height). The chevron is the only thing on screen while closed, so a
  student can see there is more without being handed it.
- `Drill.tsx` owns `breakdownOpen: boolean`, reset to `false` whenever
  Explanation turns on or the qid changes.
- Shared helper `resolveBreakdownSteps(data, activeStyle)` in
  `useLrExplanation.ts` carries the existing borrow-Standard rule (a
  non-Standard style with zero steps renders Standard's, labelled "same as
  Standard"), moved out of `DrillExplanationTab` unchanged. The rule is
  today's shipped behaviour and the label is honest about it; whether the
  ingest recorded "(same as Standard)" literally is checked during the
  build and noted in the helper's comment either way.
- `OfficialQuestionPane` gets `belowStimulus?: React.ReactNode`. The slot is
  a **sibling** of the stimulus `div`, inside a new left-column wrapper

### 10. Census of questions with no authored explanation

- `scripts/missing-explanations-census.mjs`. Loads the service key the way
  `scripts/db-pull.mjs` does, and **pages every table at 1000 rows with
  `Range` headers until a short page**, the same loop `db-pull.mjs` uses.
  Aborts (non-zero exit, nothing written) on any query error or if a table's
  row count disagrees with a `count=exact` head request.
- Reads the bank from `public/data/PT*-LR.json` (156 files).
- **One predicate, two runtimes, one fixture set.** `hasAuthoredProse` (a
  step or choice-note `body` non-empty after `trim()`) is implemented in
  `useLrExplanation.ts` (replacing the row-count `hasAuthored`) and in the
  script. A vitest spec `explanationProse.fixtures.test.ts` runs **both**
  against the same fixtures (`src/lib/__fixtures__/explanationProse.json`:
  empty, whitespace-only, null, mixed-style, note-only, step-only) by
  importing the `.mjs` predicate directly, so they cannot drift.
- Reasons are exactly what the data proves: `not ingested` (no
  `lr_questions` row for the address) or `no authored prose` (row exists,
  nothing passes the predicate). No "video only" label: nothing in the
  schema records a video; the 14 known video-only questions (PT143, PT146
  per the 2026-08-03 migration) are a subset of "no authored prose", noted
  in the report header.
- Output `ai-os/wiki/explanations/MISSING-EXPLANATIONS.md`: summary counts
  (bank total, ingested, with prose, missing by reason), then one table per
  PrepTest, rows `PT · S · Q · question type · reason`, sorted PT, section,
  question. Run once, committed to the vault, not the app repo.

## Key decisions & tradeoffs

- **Classify by launch kind, not `mode`.** ADR 0016 says `mode` is not the
  run kind; `isPracticeSetMode` is mutable state set mid-init. A synchronous
  policy from the spec is the only thing that cannot misclassify a frame.
- **No freeze, by Joshua's ruling.** The choices stay live after a
  reveal. The reveal-then-switch case Codex raised is handled by the
  record, not by the UI: the attempt carries `answerRevealed: true` and the
  change-of-mind trail, so a switch after seeing the answer is visible in
  the data. Locking or shadow-tracking the pick was rejected as the rigid
  behaviour this plan exists to remove.
- **The Explanation pill never opens the drawer.** Joshua's ruling and the
  recording. The drawer's tab shows the content only while Explanation is
  on and is otherwise an honest placeholder.
- **One synchronous action lock, not per-qid pending state.** A ref is
  tested before the first `await`; state only rerenders the disabled
  controls. One question is on screen at a time.
- **`answer_revealed` is persisted.** The fact that the answer was seen
  must survive a refresh or the record lies.
- **Fixed drills keep their bar.** It already has the arrows and ovals; only
  the Submit pill and the auto-reveal switch go.
- **Flag gates mounting, not just state.** Round 1 showed the state-only
  version leaked through the legacy controls.
- **Answer and Explanation decoupled at the state level**, only in the
  `reviewControlsAvailable` modes, with today's code as the else branch.
- **Failures stop the caller chain and are reported structurally.**
- **Pane changes are opt-in props.** `choicesStayLive`, `belowStimulus`,
  `onResetResponse`, `interactionLocked` all default to today's behaviour.
- **Everything under Explanation starts closed behind a chevron:** the
  Argument Breakdown on a rule under the stimulus, each Choice Note at the
  right edge of its card. Chevron only where there is prose.
- **The pills never ask and never commit.** No confirm dialog, no
  auto-commit of a pick; the record (`answerRevealed`) carries the fact.
- **Census predicate is fixture-tested in both runtimes.**
- **Flag, not deletion** of the tutor. Joshua wants it back later.

## Risks / open questions

- `showSolution` / `showReviewButton` remain referenced by the legacy
  layout; the audit may find the official surface still reads them.
- Breakdown label sizing vs LSAT Lab is a taste call; verify on screen.
- The census needs the service key from `~/ai-os/.env`; if absent it says
  so and exits non-zero.

import { describe, expect, it } from 'vitest';
import type { DrillRunSpec } from './drillRunSpec';
import { hasSelectedQidList, reviewPolicyForRun, pickModelForRun } from './drillReviewPolicy';

const kinds = ['guided-drill', 'assignment', 'saved-set', 'questions', 'practice-set', 'bootcamp-day', 'filter'] as const;

describe('Drill review policy', () => {
  it.each(kinds)('%s is fixed', (launchKind) => {
    expect(reviewPolicyForRun({ launchKind } as DrillRunSpec)).toBe('fixed');
  });

  it('keeps Smart Drill adaptive and sections outside review controls', () => {
    expect(reviewPolicyForRun({ launchKind: 'smart-drill' } as DrillRunSpec)).toBe('adaptive');
    expect(reviewPolicyForRun({ launchKind: 'section' } as DrillRunSpec)).toBe('none');
  });

  it('keeps sealed and practice-test runs outside review controls', () => {
    expect(reviewPolicyForRun({ launchKind: 'assignment', sealed: true } as DrillRunSpec)).toBe('none');
    expect(reviewPolicyForRun({ launchKind: 'questions', ptRun: {} as never } as DrillRunSpec)).toBe('none');
  });

  it('uses the list pick model only for a type drill with selected qids', () => {
    const list = { launchKind: 'questions', mode: 'type-drill', config: { selectedQids: ['q1'] } } as DrillRunSpec;
    const natural = { ...list, mode: 'natural-drill' } as DrillRunSpec;
    expect(hasSelectedQidList(list)).toBe(true);
    expect(pickModelForRun(list)).toBe('list');
    expect(hasSelectedQidList(natural)).toBe(false);
    expect(pickModelForRun(natural)).toBe('commit');
  });
});

// "preview" is the pre-submission structural breakdown (Conclusion / Evidence /
// Evaluate / Goal), answer-blind. The other three are the live coaching modes.
export type TutorMode = "preview" | "pre-answer" | "socratic" | "post-answer";

export function resolveTutorMode({
  userAnswer,
  answerRevealed,
}: {
  userAnswer: string | null | undefined;
  answerRevealed: boolean;
}): TutorMode {
  if (answerRevealed) return "post-answer";
  // Before an answer is committed, the Explanation button opens the structural
  // preview rather than streaming coaching.
  return userAnswer ? "socratic" : "preview";
}

export function resolveTutorLaunch({
  tutorModeEnabled,
  correct,
  answerRevealed,
}: {
  tutorModeEnabled: boolean;
  correct: boolean;
  answerRevealed: boolean;
}): { shouldOpen: boolean; mode: TutorMode } {
  return {
    shouldOpen: tutorModeEnabled && !correct,
    mode: resolveTutorMode({ userAnswer: "submitted", answerRevealed }),
  };
}
import * as React from "react";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

/**
 * The per-choice explanation layer that sits directly beneath an answer choice
 * in `OfficialQuestionPane`. Replaces the old dark `ExplanationPanel` sheet.
 *
 * The explanation is ALWAYS VISIBLE. It used to sit behind a "Why this is
 * wrong" disclosure, which cost a click per choice and, on a shared screen
 * during teaching, hid the very thing being taught. Reading five choices meant
 * five clicks. The prose is the point of the review screen, so it is set at
 * reading size and shown outright.
 *
 * Two rules carried over from that panel, and tightened to apply per choice
 * rather than per question:
 *  - Never invent content. A missing tag, percentage, or note renders as
 *    absent. A choice with no note gets no disclosure row at all — not an
 *    empty one, not a placeholder.
 *  - The source's nuance lives in the tag chip verbatim ("Too Weak", "Weakens,
 *    if anything"). The disclosure label only says which way the choice went.
 */

export interface InlineChoiceNote {
  /** Wrong-answer type from the source, e.g. "Out of Scope". */
  tag?: string | null;
  /** Percent of students who picked this choice. */
  studentPercent?: number | null;
  /** The choice's explanation prose. Absent for letters the source skipped. */
  note?: string | null;
}

/** Per-letter inline content for one question. A letter may legitimately be absent. */
export type InlineChoiceNotes = Record<string, InlineChoiceNote>;

interface Props {
  letter: string;
  isCorrect: boolean;
  /** The student's own pick, marked so they can find it fast. */
  isSelected: boolean;
  content: InlineChoiceNote;
}

/** True when there is nothing real to render for this letter. */
export function isInlineChoiceEmpty(content: InlineChoiceNote | undefined, isSelected: boolean) {
  if (isSelected) return false;
  if (!content) return true;
  return !content.tag && content.studentPercent == null && !content.note;
}

export default function InlineChoiceExplanation({
  letter,
  isCorrect,
  isSelected,
  content,
}: Props) {
  const { tag, studentPercent, note } = content;
  const hasNote = typeof note === "string" && note.trim().length > 0;

  if (isInlineChoiceEmpty(content, isSelected)) return null;

  return (
    <div className="mt-2.5">
      {/* Tag, percentage, and the student's own pick. Any of these may be absent. */}
      <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center gap-2 text-[12px]">
        {tag && (
          <span
            className={cn(
              "rounded-full px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-semibold",
              isCorrect ? "bg-green-600 text-white" : "bg-slate-200 text-slate-700",
            )}
          >
            {tag}
          </span>
        )}
        {studentPercent != null && (
          <span className="text-slate-500 tabular-nums">{studentPercent}% of students</span>
        )}
        {isSelected && (
          <span className="rounded-full border border-slate-300 px-2 py-0.5 text-[11px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide text-slate-500">
            Your answer
          </span>
        )}
      </div>

      {/* No note means nothing here. The absence is the honest render. */}
      {hasNote && (
        <div
          className={cn(
            // A left rule in the choice's own colour anchors the prose to the
            // choice it belongs to. Without it, five explanations down a page
            // read as one undifferentiated column of text.
            "mt-2 border-l-2 pl-3",
            isCorrect ? "border-green-600/50" : "border-slate-300",
          )}
        >
          <p
            className={cn(
              "text-[12px] font-semibold uppercase tracking-wide",
              isCorrect ? "text-green-800" : "text-slate-500",
            )}
          >
            {isCorrect ? "Why this is correct" : "Why this is wrong"}
          </p>
          {/* Reading size, not caption size: this is the sentence the student
              is meant to actually read, and it carries the teaching. */}
          <p className="mt-1 whitespace-pre-line text-[15px] leading-7 text-slate-800">
            {note}
          </p>
        </div>
      )}
    </div>
  );
}
import * as React from "react";
import { HelpCircle } from "lucide-react";
import { cn } from "@/lib/utils";

interface BreadcrumbItem {
  label: string;
  onClick?: () => void;
}

interface Props {
  breadcrumbs: BreadcrumbItem[];
  isPaused?: boolean;
  onTogglePause?: () => void;
  onComplete?: () => void;
  /**
   * Show the Answer pill. Reveals the correct letter plus the inline
   * explanation layer; pressed before an attempt it is the escape hatch and
   * the caller confirms the question will not be scored.
   *
   * Both this and Explanation are toggles over the same reveal state: pressed
   * again they put the question back exactly as it was, with nothing revealed.
   * A reveal used to be a one-way door, which made the pills useless for
   * teaching — showing a class the answer meant the question was spent.
   */
  showAnswerVisible?: boolean;
  onShowAnswer?: () => void;
  /** Disabled while paused, or where revealing mid-section defeats the exercise. */
  showAnswerDisabled?: boolean;
  /** True while the answer is revealed — flips the pill to its "hide" state. */
  showAnswerActive?: boolean;
  /** Opens the question drawer on the Explanation tab. Hidden if omitted. */
  onExplanation?: () => void;
  /** Label for the explanation pill (context-aware). */
  explanationLabel?: string;
  /** Disabled until something is revealed, so the breakdown cannot leak. */
  explanationDisabled?: boolean;
  /** True while the explanation layer is up — flips the pill to its "hide" state. */
  explanationActive?: boolean;
  /** Noun used in pause/complete labels: "Section" (default) or "Question". */
  pauseNoun?: string;
  /** Label for the complete/end action. Defaults to the LawHub-style full-section wording. */
  completeLabel?: string;
  /**
   * Opens the Help sheet. Lives here rather than as a floating bubble: the FAB
   * sat over the question card's bottom-right corner, which is the runner's
   * content, not its chrome. Hidden if omitted.
   */
  onOpenHelp?: () => void;
}

/** Shared gray pill. Narrow padding on small screens so the cluster still fits. */
const PILL_CLASS =
  "rounded-full bg-slate-100 hover:bg-slate-200 border border-slate-300 text-blue-900 px-3 sm:px-5 py-1.5 text-[13px] font-medium whitespace-nowrap transition-colors disabled:opacity-40 disabled:cursor-not-allowed disabled:hover:bg-slate-100";

/** A pill whose state is currently on, so pressing it again undoes it. */
const ACTIVE_PILL_CLASS =
  "bg-[#7f1d1d] hover:bg-[#991b1b] border-[#7f1d1d] text-white";

/**
 * Content of the white global header bar. Breadcrumb on the left,
 * gray pill action buttons on the right.
 */
export default function OfficialSessionStrip({
  breadcrumbs,
  isPaused,
  onTogglePause,
  onComplete,
  showAnswerVisible,
  onShowAnswer,
  showAnswerDisabled,
  showAnswerActive,
  onExplanation,
  explanationLabel = "Explanation",
  explanationDisabled,
  explanationActive,
  pauseNoun = "Section",
  completeLabel = "Complete Section",
  onOpenHelp,
}: Props) {
  return (
    <div className="flex flex-wrap items-center justify-between gap-x-4 gap-y-2 w-full text-[14px]">
      {/* Left: breadcrumb */}
      <nav aria-label="Breadcrumb" className="flex items-center gap-2 min-w-0 flex-1 truncate text-[15px]">
        {breadcrumbs.map((bc, i) => {
          const isLast = i === breadcrumbs.length - 1;
          const isClickable = !isLast && !!bc.onClick;
          return (
            <React.Fragment key={`${bc.label}-${i}`}>
          {i > 0 && <span aria-hidden className="text-slate-300">/</span>}
              {isClickable ? (
                <button
                  type="button"
                  onClick={bc.onClick}
                  className="text-blue-700 hover:text-blue-800 font-semibold transition-colors"
                >
                  {bc.label}
                </button>
              ) : (
                <span
                  className={isLast ? "text-[#7f1d1d] font-semibold" : "text-blue-700 font-semibold"}
                  aria-current={isLast ? "page" : undefined}
                >
                  {bc.label}
                </span>
              )}
            </React.Fragment>
          );
        })}
      </nav>

      {/* Right: gray pill buttons. Answer and Explanation live here rather than
          in the bottom bar — four controls crowded that bar's left corner, and
          these two are review actions, the same register as Pause. */}
      <div className="flex items-center gap-2 shrink-0">
        {showAnswerVisible && onShowAnswer && (
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={onShowAnswer}
            disabled={showAnswerDisabled}
            aria-pressed={!!showAnswerActive}
            title={showAnswerActive ? "Hide the answer and reset the question" : undefined}
            className={cn(PILL_CLASS, showAnswerActive && ACTIVE_PILL_CLASS)}
          >
            {showAnswerActive ? "Hide Answer" : "Answer"}
          </button>
        )}
        {onExplanation && (
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={onExplanation}
            disabled={explanationDisabled}
            aria-pressed={!!explanationActive}
            title={
              explanationActive
                ? "Collapse the explanations and reset the question"
                : "Reveal the answer and every choice's explanation"
            }
            className={cn(PILL_CLASS, explanationActive && ACTIVE_PILL_CLASS)}
          >
            {explanationActive ? `Hide ${explanationLabel}` : explanationLabel}
          </button>
        )}
        {onOpenHelp && (
          <button
            type="button"
            onClick={onOpenHelp}
            aria-label="Get help from Joshua"
            title="Get help from Joshua"
            className="rounded-full bg-slate-100 hover:bg-slate-200 border border-slate-300 text-blue-900 h-[30px] w-[30px] inline-flex items-center justify-center transition-colors"
          >
            <HelpCircle className="w-4 h-4" />
          </button>
        )}
        {onTogglePause && (
          <button type="button" onClick={onTogglePause} className={PILL_CLASS}>
            {isPaused ? `Resume ${pauseNoun}` : `Pause ${pauseNoun}`}
          </button>
        )}
        {onComplete && (
          <button type="button" onClick={() => onComplete()} className={PILL_CLASS}>
            {completeLabel}
          </button>
        )}
      </div>
    </div>
  );
}

import { DrillLaunchError, type DrillLaunch, type PtLegRef } from '@/lib/drillLaunch';
import type { DrillResumeIdentity } from '@/lib/drillResumeSession';

export interface DrillRunSpec {
  mode?: DrillMode;
  config?: FullSectionConfig | TypeDrillConfig;
  /** Heading shown while the drill runs. */
  practicingLabel?: string;
  /** Where the runner's exit sends the student. Always app-relative. */
  returnTo?: string;
  /** True when the runner is launched from a Guided Drill. */
  isGuidedDrill?: boolean;
  guidedDurationMinutes?: number;
  /**

   */
  present?: 'smart';
  /**
   * Seals the run: Answer and Explanation are not rendered at all, for the
   * whole session, for every question. A launch property and not a student
   * setting, because the person who assigns the section decides.
   */
  sealed?: boolean;
  /** Open on this question rather than the first. */
  initialIndex?: number;

  guidedAssignmentId?: string;

  practiceSource?: string;
  practiceSlug?: string;
  /** Names the Run Record for a bootcamp day, so a re-draw cannot orphan it. */
  resumeIdentity?: DrillResumeIdentity;
  /** Which practice-test run and leg this section closes out. */
  ptRun?: PtLegRef;
}

export interface DrillTimerConfig {
  mode: 'countdown' | 'stopwatch';
  durationMs?: number;
}

function typeDrillConfig(qids: string[]): TypeDrillConfig {
  return {
    qtypes: [],
    difficulties: [],
    pts: [],
    count: qids.length,
    selectedQids: qids,
  };
}

const QID_PATTERN = /^PT(\d+)-S(\d+)-Q\d+$/;

/**
 * A homework set built from one real section — every qid comes from the same
 * PT and section number, the shape `assign.ts` and the admin set builder both
 * produce today. Such a set is a section wearing a homework row, not a mixed
 * question list, so it runs under a real countdown instead of the untimed
 * stopwatch every other homework set gets. A set that mixes sections (or PTs)
 * returns null and keeps the untimed behaviour, because there is no one clock
 * that honestly times a blend.
 */
export function singleSectionOf(qids: string[]): { pt: number; section: number } | null {
  if (qids.length === 0) return null;
  let pt: number | null = null;
  let section: number | null = null;
  for (const qid of qids) {
    const match = QID_PATTERN.exec(qid);
    if (!match) return null;
    const qPt = Number(match[1]);
    const qSection = Number(match[2]);
    if (pt === null) {
      pt = qPt;
      section = qSection;
    } else if (qPt !== pt || qSection !== section) {
      return null;
    }
  }
  return pt !== null && section !== null ? { pt, section } : null;
}

    initialIndex: options.initialIndex,
  };

    case 'smart-drill':
      return { ...base, mode: 'adaptive', sealed: false };

    case 'section': {
